[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/WorldPopulation.png/220px-WorldPopulation.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:WorldPopulation.png)

world population

East Asia had by far the strongest overall [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index "Human Development Index") (HDI) improvement of any region in the world, nearly doubling average HDI attainment over the past 40 years, according to the report's analysis of health, education and income data. China, the second highest achiever in the world in terms of HDI improvement since

1970, is the only country on the "Top 10 Movers" list due to income rather than health or education achievements. Its per capita income increased a stunning 21-fold over the last four decades, also lifting hundreds of millions out of income poverty. Yet it was not among the region's top performers in improving school enrollment and life expectancy.[\[122\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-2010_Human_Development_Report-125)\
[Nepal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nepal "Nepal"), a South Asian country, emerges as one of the world's fastest movers since 1970 mainly due to health and education achievements. Its present [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy "Life expectancy") is 25 years longer than in the 1970s. More than four of every five children of school age in Nepal now attend primary school, compared to just one in five 40 years ago.[\[122\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-2010_Human_Development_Report-125)\
Hong Kong ranked highest among the countries grouped on the HDI (number 7 in the world, which is in the "very high human development" category), followed by Singapore (9), Japan (19) and South Korea (22). [Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghanistan "Afghanistan") (155) ranked lowest amongst Asian countries out of the 169 countries assessed.[\[122\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-2010_Human_Development_Report-125)

### Languages

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Asia\&action=edit\&section=20 "Edit section: Languages")]

Main article: [Languages of Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Asia "Languages of Asia")

Asia is home to several [language families](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_families "Language families") and many [language isolates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_isolate "Language isolate"). Most Asian countries have more than one language that is natively spoken. For instance, according to [Ethnologue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnologue "Ethnologue"), more than 700 languages are spoken in Indonesia, more than 400 languages spoken in India, and more than 100 are spoken in the Philippines. China has many languages and dialects in different provinces.

### Religions

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Asia\&action=edit\&section=21 "Edit section: Religions")]

See also: [Eastern philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_philosophy "Eastern philosophy"), [Religion in Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Asia "Religion in Asia"), and [List of Asian mythologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Asian_mythologies "List of Asian mythologies")

Many of the world's [major religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_religions "Major religions") have their origins in Asia, including the five most practiced in the world (excluding [irreligion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion "Irreligion")), which are Christianity, Islam, Hinduism, Chinese folk religion (classified as Confucianism and Taoism), and Buddhism. Asian mythology is complex and diverse. The story of the [Great Flood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Flood "Great Flood") for example, as presented to Jews in the [Hebrew Bible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_Bible "Hebrew Bible") in the narrative of [Noah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noah "Noah")—and later to Christians in the [Old Testament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Testament "Old Testament"), and to Muslims in the [Quran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quran "Quran")—is earliest found in [Mesopotamian mythology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesopotamian_mythology "Mesopotamian mythology"), in the [Enûma Eliš](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/En%C3%BBma_Eli%C5%A1 "Enûma Eliš") and *[Epic of Gilgamesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epic_of_Gilgamesh "Epic of Gilgamesh")*. [Hindu mythology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_mythology "Hindu mythology") similarly tells about an [avatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avatar "Avatar") of [Vishnu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vishnu "Vishnu") in the form of a [fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matsya "Matsya") who warned [Manu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sraddhadeva_Manu "Sraddhadeva Manu") of a terrible flood. Ancient [Chinese mythology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_mythology "Chinese mythology") also tells of a [Great Flood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Flood_\(China\) "Great Flood (China)") spanning generations, one that required the combined efforts of emperors and divinities to control.

#### Abrahamic

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Asia\&action=edit\&section=22 "Edit section: Abrahamic")]

See also: [Christianity in Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Asia "Christianity in Asia") and [Islam in Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Asia "Islam in Asia")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Westernwall2.jpg/220px-Westernwall2.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Westernwall2.jpg)

The [Western Wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Wall "Western Wall") and the [Dome of the Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dome_of_the_Rock "Dome of the Rock"), [Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem "Jerusalem")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Church_of_the_Nativity_%287703592746%29.jpg/220px-Church_of_the_Nativity_%287703592746%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Church_of_the_Nativity_\(7703592746\).jpg)

The [Church of the Nativity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_the_Nativity "Church of the Nativity") in [Bethlehem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bethlehem "Bethlehem")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Kaaba_mirror_edit_jj.jpg/220px-Kaaba_mirror_edit_jj.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Kaaba_mirror_edit_jj.jpg)

Pilgrims in the annual [Hajj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hajj "Hajj") at the [Kaabah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaabah "Kaabah") in [Mecca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mecca "Mecca")

The [Abrahamic religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abrahamic_religions "Abrahamic religions") including [Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaism "Judaism"), [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity "Christianity"), [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam "Islam"), [Druze faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze_faith "Druze faith"),[\[123\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-126) and [Baháʼí Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith "Baháʼí Faith") originated in West Asia.[\[124\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-127)[\[125\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-128)

[Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaism "Judaism"), the oldest of the Abrahamic faiths, is practiced primarily in [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel "Israel"), the indigenous homeland and historical birthplace of the [Hebrew nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jews "Jews"): which today consists both of those [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jews "Jews") who remained in [the Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mizrahi_Jews "Mizrahi Jews") and those who returned from [diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_diaspora "Jewish diaspora") in Europe, North America, and other regions;[\[126\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-129) though various diaspora communities persist worldwide. Jews are the predominant ethnic group in [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel "Israel") (75.6%) numbering at about 6.1 million,[\[127\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-130) although the levels of adherence to Jewish religion vary. Outside of Israel there are small ancient Jewish communities in [Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey "Turkey") (17,400),[\[128\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-131) [Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan "Azerbaijan") (9,100),[\[129\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-132) Iran (8,756),[\[130\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-133) India (5,000) and [Uzbekistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uzbekistan "Uzbekistan") (4,000),[\[131\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-134) among many other places. As of 2016, there are am estimated 14.4–17.5 million (2016, est.)[\[132\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-135) Jews alive in the world today, making them one of the smallest Asian minorities, at roughly 0.3–0.4& of the total population of the continent.

[Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity "Christianity") is a widespread religion in Asia, with more than 286 million adherents in 2010 according to [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center "Pew Research Center"),[\[133\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-136) and nearly 364 million according to [Britannica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britannica "Britannica") Book of the Year 2014.[\[134\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-137) Christians constitute around 12.6% of the total population of Asia. In the Philippines and [East Timor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Timor "East Timor"), [Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholicism "Catholicism") is the predominant religion;[\[135\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Pew_Research_Center-138) it was introduced by the Spaniards and the Portuguese, respectively. In [Armenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenia "Armenia") and Georgia, [Eastern Orthodoxy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy "Eastern Orthodoxy") is the predominant religion.[\[135\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Pew_Research_Center-138) In the Middle East, such as in the [Levant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levant "Levant"), [Anatolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anatolia "Anatolia") and [Fars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fars_\(territory\) "Fars (territory)"), [Syriac Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syriac_Christianity "Syriac Christianity") ([Church of the East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_the_East "Church of the East")) and [Oriental Orthodoxy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oriental_Orthodoxy "Oriental Orthodoxy") are prevalent minority denominations,[\[136\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-139) which are both [Eastern Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Christian "Eastern Christian") sects mainly adhered to [Assyrian people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyrian_people "Assyrian people") or Syriac Christians. Vibrant indigenous minorities in [West Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Asia "West Asia") are adhering to the [Eastern Catholic Churches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Catholic_Churches "Eastern Catholic Churches") and [Eastern Orthodoxy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy "Eastern Orthodoxy").[\[135\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Pew_Research_Center-138) [Saint Thomas Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians "Saint Thomas Christians") in India trace their origins to the evangelistic activity of [Thomas the Apostle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_the_Apostle "Thomas the Apostle") in the 1st century.[\[137\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-140) Significant Christian communities also found in [Central Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Asia "Central Asia"), [South Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asia "South Asia"), [Southeast Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Asia "Southeast Asia") and [East Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Asia "East Asia").[\[135\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Pew_Research_Center-138)

[Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam "Islam"), which originated in the [Hejaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hejaz "Hejaz") located in modern-day Saudi Arabia, is the second largest and most widely-spread religion in Asia with at least 1 billion Muslims constituting around 23.8% of the total population of Asia.[\[138\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-141) With 12.7% of the world Muslim population, the country currently with the largest Muslim population in the world is Indonesia, followed by Pakistan (11.5%), India (10%), Bangladesh, Iran and Turkey. [Mecca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mecca "Mecca"), [Medina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medina "Medina") and [Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holiest_sites_in_Islam#Jerusalem "Holiest sites in Islam") are the three holiest cities for Islam in all the world. The [Hajj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hajj "Hajj") and [Umrah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umrah "Umrah") attract large numbers of Muslim devotees from all over the world to Mecca and Medina. Iran is the largest [Shia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shia "Shia") country.

The [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze "Druze") originated in West Asia, is a monotheistic religion based on the teachings of figures like [Hamza ibn Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamza_ibn_Ali "Hamza ibn Ali") and [al-Hakim bi-Amr Allah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Hakim_bi-Amr_Allah "Al-Hakim bi-Amr Allah"), and Greek [philosophers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philosophers "Philosophers") such as [Plato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plato "Plato") and [Aristotle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aristotle "Aristotle"). The number of [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze "Druze") people worldwide is around one million. Around 45–50% live in [Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syria "Syria"), 35% to 40% live in [Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon "Lebanon"), and less than 10% live in [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel "Israel"). Recently there has been a growing Druze diaspora.[\[139\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-142)

The [Baháʼí Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith "Baháʼí Faith") originated in Asia, in Iran (Persia), and spread from there to the Ottoman Empire, Central Asia, India, and Burma during the lifetime of [Bahá'u'lláh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%27u%27ll%C3%A1h "Bahá'u'lláh"). Since the middle of the 20th century, growth has particularly occurred in other Asian countries, because Baháʼí activities in many Muslim countries has been [severely suppressed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%ADs "Persecution of Baháʼís") by authorities. [Lotus Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lotus_Temple "Lotus Temple") is a big [Baháʼí temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_temple "Baháʼí temple") in India.

#### Indian and East Asian religions

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Asia\&action=edit\&section=23 "Edit section: Indian and East Asian religions")]

See also: [Eastern religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_religions "Eastern religions")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/Akshardham_Lotus.jpg/220px-Akshardham_Lotus.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Akshardham_Lotus.jpg)

The [Swaminarayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swaminarayan_Hinduism "Swaminarayan Hinduism") [Akshardham Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akshardham_Temple "Akshardham Temple") in [Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi "Delhi"), according to the [Guinness World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_World_Record "Guinness World Record"), is the *World's Largest Comprehensive Hindu Temple*.[\[140\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-143)

Almost all Asian religions have philosophical character and Asian philosophical traditions cover a large spectrum of philosophical thoughts and writings. [Indian philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_philosophy "Indian philosophy") includes [Hindu philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_philosophy "Hindu philosophy") and [Buddhist philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist_philosophy "Buddhist philosophy"). They include elements of nonmaterial pursuits, whereas another school of thought from India, [Cārvāka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C4%81rv%C4%81ka "Cārvāka"), preached the enjoyment of the material world. The religions of [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism "Hinduism"), [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism "Buddhism"), [Jainism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism "Jainism") and [Sikhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikhism "Sikhism") originated in India, South Asia. In East Asia, particularly in China and Japan, [Confucianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucianism "Confucianism"), [Taoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoism "Taoism") and [Zen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zen "Zen") Buddhism took shape.

As of 2012[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Asia\&action=edit), Hinduism has around 1.1 billion adherents. The faith represents around 25% of Asia's population and is the largest religion in Asia. However, it is mostly concentrated in South Asia. Over 80% of the populations of both India and Nepal adhere to Hinduism, alongside significant communities in Bangladesh, Pakistan, Bhutan, Sri Lanka and [Bali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bali "Bali"), Indonesia. Many overseas Indians in countries such as Burma, Singapore and Malaysia also adhere to Hinduism.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ef/Angkor_Wat_reflejado_en_un_estanque_02.jpg/220px-Angkor_Wat_reflejado_en_un_estanque_02.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Angkor_Wat_reflejado_en_un_estanque_02.jpg)

The Angkor Wat in Cambodia, a Hindu-Buddhist temple and the largest religious monument in the world

Buddhism has a great following in mainland Southeast Asia and East Asia. Buddhism is the religion of the majority of the populations of Cambodia (96%),[\[141\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-144) [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thailand "Thailand") (95%),[\[142\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-145) [Burma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burma "Burma") (80–89%),[\[143\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-146) Japan (36–96%),[\[144\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-147) [Bhutan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhutan "Bhutan") (75–84%),[\[145\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-148) [Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka "Sri Lanka") (70%),[\[146\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-149) [Laos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laos "Laos") (60–67%)[\[147\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-150) and [Mongolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongolia "Mongolia") (53–93%).[\[148\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-151) [Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan "Taiwan") (35–93%),[\[149\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-152)[\[150\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-153)[\[151\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-154)[\[152\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-155) South Korea (23–50%),[\[153\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-156) [Malaysia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaysia "Malaysia") (19–21%),[\[154\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-157) [Nepal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nepal "Nepal") (9–11%),[\[155\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-158) [Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam "Vietnam") (10–75%),[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-159) China (20–50%),[\[157\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-160) [North Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Korea "North Korea") (2–14%),[\[158\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-161)[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-162)[\[160\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-163) and small communities in India and Bangladesh. The Communist-governed countries of China, Vietnam and North Korea are officially atheist, thus the number of Buddhists and other religious adherents may be under-reported.

[Jainism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism "Jainism") is found mainly in India and in overseas Indian communities such as the United States and Malaysia. [Sikhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikhism "Sikhism") is found in Northern India and amongst overseas Indian communities in other parts of Asia, especially Southeast Asia. [Confucianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucianism "Confucianism") is found predominantly in mainland China, South Korea, Taiwan and in overseas Chinese populations. [Taoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoism "Taoism") is found mainly in mainland China, Taiwan, Malaysia and Singapore. In many Chinese communities, Taoism is easily syncretised with [Mahayana Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahayana_Buddhism "Mahayana Buddhism"), thus exact religious statistics are difficult to obtain and may be understated or overstated.

## Culture

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Asia\&action=edit\&section=24 "Edit section: Culture")]

Main article: [Culture of Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Asia "Culture of Asia")

The [culture of Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Asia "Culture of Asia") is a diverse blend of customs and traditions that have been practiced by the various ethnic groups of the continent for centuries. The continent is divided into six geographic sub-regions: [Central Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Asia "Central Asia"), [East Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Asian_cultural_sphere "East Asian cultural sphere"), [North Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Asia "North Asia"), [South Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asian_ethnic_groups "South Asian ethnic groups"), [Southeast Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Asia "Southeast Asia"), and [West Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Asia "West Asia").[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-164) These regions are defined by their cultural similarities, including common religions, languages, and ethnicities. West Asia, also known as Southwest Asia or the [Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East "Middle East"), has cultural roots in the ancient civilisations of the [Fertile Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fertile_Crescent "Fertile Crescent") and [Mesopotamia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesopotamia "Mesopotamia"), which gave rise to the [Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran "Iran"), [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_world "Arab world"), [Ottoman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Empire "Ottoman Empire") empires, as well as the Abrahamic religions of Judaism, \[Christianity and Islam.[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-165) These civilisations, which are located in the [Hilly flanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilly_flanks "Hilly flanks"), are among the oldest in the world, with evidence of farming dating back to around 9000 BCE.[\[163\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-166) Despite the challenges posed by the vast size of the continent and the presence of natural barriers such as deserts and mountain ranges, trade and commerce have helped to create a [Pan-Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-Asian "Pan-Asian") culture that is shared across the region.[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-167)

### Nobel laureates

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Asia\&action=edit\&section=25 "Edit section: Nobel laureates")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Rabindranath_Tagore_in_1909.jpg/180px-Rabindranath_Tagore_in_1909.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Rabindranath_Tagore_in_1909.jpg)

Nobel laureate

[Rabindranath Tagore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabindranath_Tagore "Rabindranath Tagore"), a [Bengali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_literature "Bengali literature") dramatist and author from [Santiniketan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiniketan "Santiniketan") (now in [West Bengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bengal "West Bengal"), India), won the [Nobel Prize in Literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_Prize_in_Literature "Nobel Prize in Literature") in 1913, becoming the first Asian [Nobel laureate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_laureate "Nobel laureate"). The prize was awarded for Tagore's prose works and poetry, which had a significant additional impact on national literatures throughout the Western world. Tagore also authored both the Indian and Bangladeshi national anthems.

Other Asian writers who won Nobel Prize for literature include [Yasunari Kawabata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasunari_Kawabata "Yasunari Kawabata") (Japan, 1968), [Kenzaburō Ōe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenzabur%C5%8D_%C5%8Ce "Kenzaburō Ōe") (Japan, 1994), [Gao Xingjian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gao_Xingjian "Gao Xingjian") (China, 2000), [Orhan Pamuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orhan_Pamuk "Orhan Pamuk") (Turkey, 2006), and [Mo Yan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mo_Yan "Mo Yan") (China, 2012). Some may consider the American writer, [Pearl S. Buck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearl_S._Buck "Pearl S. Buck"), an honorary Asian Nobel laureate, having spent considerable time in China as the daughter of missionaries, and based many of her novels, namely *[The Good Earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Good_Earth "The Good Earth")* (1931) and *[The Mother](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Mother_\(1934_novel\) "The Mother (1934 novel)")* (1933), as well as the biographies of her parents for their time in China, *[The Exile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Exile_\(1936_book\) "The Exile (1936 book)")* and *[Fighting Angel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fighting_Angel "Fighting Angel")*, all of which earned her the Literature prize in 1938.

[Mother Teresa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_Teresa "Mother Teresa") of India and [Shirin Ebadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shirin_Ebadi "Shirin Ebadi") of Iran were awarded the Nobel Peace Prize for their significant and pioneering efforts for [democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy "Democracy") and human rights, especially for the rights of women and children. Ebadi is the first Iranian and the first Muslim woman to receive the prize. Another Nobel Peace Prize winner is [Aung San Suu Kyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aung_San_Suu_Kyi "Aung San Suu Kyi") from [Burma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burma "Burma") for her peaceful and non-violent struggle under a military dictatorship in Burma. She is a nonviolent pro-democracy activist and leader of the National League for Democracy in Burma (Myanmar) and a noted prisoner of conscience. She is a [Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist "Buddhist") and was awarded the Nobel Peace Prize in 1991. Chinese dissident [Liu Xiaobo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liu_Xiaobo "Liu Xiaobo") was awarded the Nobel Peace Prize for "his long and non-violent struggle for fundamental human rights in China" on 8 October 2010. He is the first Chinese citizen to be awarded a Nobel Prize of any kind while residing in China. In 2014, [Kailash Satyarthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kailash_Satyarthi "Kailash Satyarthi") from India and [Malala Yousafzai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malala_Yousafzai "Malala Yousafzai") from Pakistan were awarded the Nobel Peace Prize "for their struggle against the suppression of children and young people and for the right of all children to education".

[C.V. Raman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C.V._Raman "C.V. Raman") is the first Asian to get a Nobel prize in Sciences. He won the [Nobel Prize in Physics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_Prize_in_Physics "Nobel Prize in Physics") "for his work on the scattering of light and for the discovery of the [effect named after him](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raman_scattering "Raman scattering")".

Japan has won the most Nobel Prizes of any Asian nation with 24 followed by India which has won 13.\[*[needs update](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers")*]

[Amartya Sen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amartya_Sen "Amartya Sen") (b. 1933) is an Indian economist who was awarded the 1998 Nobel Memorial Prize in Economic Sciences for his contributions to welfare economics and [social choice theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_choice_theory "Social choice theory"), and for his interest in the problems of society's poorest members.

Other Asian Nobel Prize winners include [Subrahmanyan Chandrasekhar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subrahmanyan_Chandrasekhar "Subrahmanyan Chandrasekhar"), [Abdus Salam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdus_Salam "Abdus Salam"), [Robert Aumann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Aumann "Robert Aumann"), [Menachem Begin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_Begin "Menachem Begin"), [Aaron Ciechanover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_Ciechanover "Aaron Ciechanover"), [Avram Hershko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avram_Hershko "Avram Hershko"), [Daniel Kahneman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Kahneman "Daniel Kahneman"), [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres "Shimon Peres"), [Yitzhak Rabin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitzhak_Rabin "Yitzhak Rabin"), [Ada Yonath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ada_Yonath "Ada Yonath"), [Yasser Arafat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasser_Arafat "Yasser Arafat"), [José Ramos-Horta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Ramos-Horta "José Ramos-Horta") and Bishop [Carlos Filipe Ximenes Belo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Filipe_Ximenes_Belo "Carlos Filipe Ximenes Belo") of [Timor Leste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timor_Leste "Timor Leste"), [Kim Dae-jung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Dae-jung "Kim Dae-jung"), and 13 Japanese scientists. Most of the said awardees are from Japan and [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel "Israel") except for Chandrasekhar and Raman (India), Abdus Salam (Pakistan), Arafat (Palestinian Territories), Kim (South Korea), and Horta and Belo (Timor Leste).\[*[needs update](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers")*]

In 2006, the Bangladeshi [Muhammad Yunus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Yunus "Muhammad Yunus") of was awarded the Nobel Peace Prize for the establishment of [Grameen Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grameen_Bank "Grameen Bank"), a community development bank that lends money to poor people, especially women. He is known for the concept of micro credit which, allows poor and destitute people to borrow money. The borrowers pay back money within the specified period and defaulting is very low. Yunus also became the leader of an [interim government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yunus_interim_government "Yunus interim government") after the [2024 Bangladesh quota reform movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Bangladesh_quota_reform_movement "2024 Bangladesh quota reform movement").[\[165\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-168) The [Dalai Lama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalai_Lama "Dalai Lama") received the Nobel Peace Prize, in [Oslo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo "Oslo"), Norway in 1989.[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-169)

The most [democratic countries in Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy_in_Asia "Democracy in Asia") are [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan "Japan"), [Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan "Taiwan") and [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel "Israel") according to the [V-Dem Democracy indices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V-Dem_Democracy_indices "V-Dem Democracy indices") in 2024.[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-174)

## Notes

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Asia\&action=edit\&section=28 "Edit section: Notes")]

1. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-10)** Asia is normally considered its own continent in the English speaking world, which uses the seven continent model.[\[8\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-8)[\[9\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-9) Other models consider Asia as part of a Eurasian or Afro-Eurasian continent (see [Continent § Number](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continent#Number "Continent")).
2. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-13)** 44,579,000 square kilometres (17,212,000 square miles)
3. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-87)** Siberia lies in Asia geographically, but is considered part of Europe culturally and politically.
4. ^ [***a***](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-transcon_170-0) [***b***](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-transcon_170-1) [***c***](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-transcon_170-2) [***d***](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-transcon_170-3) [***e***](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-transcon_170-4) [Transcontinental country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transcontinental_country "Transcontinental country")
5. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-171)** Russia is a [transcontinental country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transcontinental_country "Transcontinental country") located in [Eastern Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Europe "Eastern Europe") and [North Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Asia "North Asia"), but is considered European historically, culturally, ethnically, and politically, and the vast majority of its population (78%) lives within its [European part](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Russia "European Russia").
6. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-172)** Moscow is located in [Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europe "Europe").
7. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-173)** Turkey is a [transcontinental country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transcontinental_country "Transcontinental country") located mainly in [West Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Asia "West Asia") with a smaller portion in [Southeastern Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeastern_Europe "Southeastern Europe").
